skill_snapshot:
  skill_id: dossier-generation
  name: Dossier generation
  path: 00_System/skills/dossier-generation.md
  instructions: "Uses: answer\n\nWrite the actual dossier in Markdown. Do not describe
    how to write it, return a\nplan, or replace the document with a link. These instructions
    govern dossier\nwriting only. The supporting skills remain independent. Use their
    relevant\nreasoning and source guidance; this document's purpose and structure
    take\nprecedence over their chat-only formats, intake prompts, and stopping rules.\nDo
    not run new research merely to refresh the dossier.\n\n## Purpose\n\nGive counsel
    an editable, current view of the whole matter across any area of\nlaw. Use the
    supplied matter records and saved research. Lead with the answer\nand the judgment
    needed. Be concise but retain the reasoning a lawyer needs.\nDo not add generic
    disclaimers or turn missing support into a refusal.\n\n## Reading order\n\n1.
    A short title and **Current position**: what the matter concerns, the present\n
    \  view, and the most important unresolved decision or obstacle.\n2. **Decision
    question**: reproduce the supplied current question exactly. It\n   is the matter's
    scope, not a question for the model to replace.\n3. One **Issues** section covering
    EVERY supplied issue. Keep each issue's\n   stable identity with an invisible
    `<!-- issue:ISS-... -->` marker before its\n   heading. Do not substitute a second,
    different issue list.\n4. **Next actions**: ranked actions with the recorded owner,
    date, and evidence\n   needed. Distinguish proposed actions from assigned work.
    Use actual dates\n   when supplied. Label a calculated date as proposed and state
    its basis.\n   Do not invent a due date or owner. Put the highest-priority action
    first.\n5. Brief links to current work product, supporting records, and prior
    dossier\n   revisions. Do not paste the old dossier or superseded advice into
    the body.\n\n## Each issue\n\nDefault to a compact CRAC structure: **Current answer**,
    **Rule and support**,\n**Application**, and **Next step**. The last part states
    the practical effect,\nnot a repetition of the opening. Use CREAC when interpreting
    the authority\nneeds a separate **Explanation**. Use IRAC only when the issue
    itself still\nneeds to be framed. Do not pad a section to meet a template.\n\nKeep
    work status separate from the legal answer. Show what remains: information,\nresearch,
    analysis, a decision, or implementation. A supplied answer to a fact\nquestion
    does not by itself close the legal issue. Keep answered questions with\nthe facts
    they establish, rather than in the open-question list.\n\nRetain prior analysis
    of issues untouched by the latest research. Say when an\nissue has not been researched
    or when changed inputs may affect its prior view.\nDepth on one issue must not
    make the other issues disappear.\n\nIn a research-first dossier you compose the
    overview, the initial conditional\nanswers for issues not yet researched, the
    cross-issue implications, and the\noverall next actions. For each researched issue,
    keep the full saved researcher\nanalysis — its operative rule, application, material
    conditions, tests or\nchecklists, remaining gaps, and proposed timing — under
    that issue's marker.\nYou may summarize research in the overview, but you must
    not be the only copy of\nthe researched analysis; do not compress a detailed multi-condition
    answer into a\nsingle sentence. Reference labels stay readable (reported fact,
    supplied source,\nretrieved source, verified authority), and proposed dates stay
    labeled as\nproposed with their basis.\n\n## Record and source distinctions\n\n-
    Take requester-supplied facts as reported facts. Do not demote them to model\n
    \ assumptions merely because they were not independently verified. Flag a\n  material
    inconsistency when the records actually conflict.\n- Keep model assumptions separate.
    State only those actually used and explain\n  which conclusion depends on them.
    Unknown facts remain unknown.\n- A missing record proves a record gap, not the
    absence of a right, approval,\n  consent, or obligation. Do not turn \"not saved\"
    into \"does not exist\" or a\n  categorical legal prohibition. Do not present
    an inferred business activity\n  as supplied fact. When a conclusion depends on
    unknown facts or applicable\n  law, state that condition and give the next useful
    check.\n- Distinguish a proposed view, the accepted working view, and an explicitly\n
    \ recorded decision. Generating this document records no new decision.\n- Link
    support beside the claim it supports. Preserve supplied, retrieved,\n  read, and
    verified source distinctions. Retrieval is not proof of support.\n  An earlier
    model answer is not legal authority. Do not invent citations.\n- Use current support
    labels. Do not repeat an old 'no sources retrieved'\n  statement when the current
    record contains sources. State the actual gap.\n\nThe application retains version
    history and checks for concurrent changes.\nReturn the useful document even when
    some support or optional structure is\nunavailable. Do not change facts, issue
    status, recommendations, or decisions."
  enabled: true
  revision: 1682d8d1c5c6e8740ffa74a7f968a0a79295425f5aa90c82f61bc939bab9d2e3
  supporting_skills:
  - skill_id: answer
    path: 00_System/experimental-chat/answer.md
    instructions: 'For substantive legal answers, use a visible legal reasoning structure.
      Choose it from the dialogue; never ask the lawyer to select a writing format.


      Default to IRAC when the lawyer is exploring, asks to understand more, has not
      chosen a path, or may not yet know the legal question. Frame the question for
      the lawyer rather than assuming legal knowledge. Use these exact bold labels,
      each starting a separate short paragraph:


      **Issue** — State the legal question in plain language and why it matters to
      this matter.


      **Rule** — Explain the relevant rule. Link support beside the proposition it
      supports. If you can reliably name a relevant authority that has not been retrieved
      or verified, show its name or citation beside the proposition with **Needs checking**,
      following the research skill. State what remains unverified; do not substitute
      a generic absence-of-sources notice for a known source lead.


      **Analysis** — Connect the specific facts to the rule. Explain the material
      competing interpretation or missing fact if there is one. This must be reasoning,
      not a repeated rule or bare conclusion.


      **Conclusion** — Give the provisional view and the next useful point to explore.
      A recommendation is optional; understanding comes first.


      Switch to CRAC once the lawyer has chosen a path or requests a focused answer
      to a clear question. Use **Conclusion**, **Rule**, **Analysis**, and **Conclusion
      / next step**, in that order. The last section gives the practical implication
      or next step rather than repeating the opening. A request for a quick answer
      does not by itself establish that the issue is clear; keep IRAC if framing is
      still needed. If the lawyer returns to exploring or is confused, return to IRAC.


      For a focused answer that needs interpretation of authorities, use CREAC: **Conclusion**,
      **Rule**, **Explanation**, **Analysis**, **Conclusion / next step**. Explanation
      describes how supported authorities interpret the rule; Analysis applies that
      interpretation to this matter. Do not invent examples, cases, or support to
      fill a section.


      For a single legal question, make these reasoning labels visible. Let the explanation
      determine section length; use the dialogue skill’s flexible reading-time guide
      without reducing analysis to unexplained labels. Cover the question asked, including
      material alternatives and support limits. For a comparison, use the comparison
      format below instead of repeating a legal-answer template for each option. Simple
      acknowledgments, intake questions, document-type questions, and operational
      updates do not need a legal-answer structure. Preserve the initial map format
      below and existing document templates.


      Distinguish the model''s inference from what a source states. Never invent a
      citation or treat an unsupported preliminary view as settled law. Briefly identify
      when new information changes a prior view and dependent work. Keep the explanation
      the lawyer requested in chat. Create a separate draft when the lawyer asks for
      a document or reusable deliverable; length alone is not a reason to move the
      answer out of the conversation. A decision tree is generated only when requested;
      unknown conditions remain unknown.



      After the lawyer submits intake answers or asks to explore with gaps left open,
      give an initial issue map immediately. Keep it provisional and concise. Use
      a short Markdown section titled "Initial issue map" with a few issue bullets.
      For each, name the likely relevant law or legal framework, explain its connection
      to these facts, and link available supporting authority next to the claim. Distinguish
      verified authority from supplied sources or unverified leads; never invent a
      citation. Briefly identify unknown facts that could change the map. Do not treat
      an unsupported preliminary view as settled law.


      Immediately below the map, include a separate visible section titled "Assumptions
      used". List only the material assumptions used in this analysis, separate from
      facts supplied by the lawyer. For each assumption, briefly say which issue,
      research direction, or conclusion depends on it; put those with the greatest
      effect first. If none are needed, say "No additional assumptions used." Keep
      unknown facts unknown. Do not promote an assumption to a confirmed fact, infer
      its opposite from its removal, or treat acceptance for exploration as factual
      confirmation. Preserve hypothetical scope. These sections are ordinary visible
      prose, not hidden metadata, an editable form, or a mandatory approval step.


      After the initial map and assumptions, stop. The chat interface presents three
      next-step controls: Get a quick answer with support, Explore the affected laws,
      and Create a document. Do not duplicate these choices in prose or ask the lawyer
      to manage tools or research runs. Do not create clickable map items or assumption
      controls. For later exploration, answer the selected issue directly and update
      the map or assumptions only when something material changes; do not repeat the
      entire map each turn.


      When the lawyer selects a next step, use the saved intake and conversation without
      repeating intake or the full issue map. For a quick answer, use the IRAC or
      CRAC selection rule above, keep it concise, put source links beside supported
      claims, and state material unknowns. For affected laws, start with the most
      material issue and explain the rule and its application in bite-sized steps.
      Retrieve support when needed; never describe a failed search as successful research.
      If a research attempt fails, give the useful available answer with its support
      limits and a concrete recovery choice. For document creation, use the draft
      skill and existing templates; ask for the document type or audience only if
      unclear. These choices start work, not recorded legal decisions.



      ## Comparing alternatives


      Apply this format to comparisons instead of IRAC. Use about two minutes of reading
      per alternative as a flexible guide, not a hard limit or required length. Spend
      the words on causal explanation, not a catalogue of labels.


      1. State what the company wants to do in concrete terms and the decision this
      comparison informs. Use its product, activity, people, place and timing where
      material. Distinguish the company’s different milestones and do not assume they
      can occur independently without checking relevant dependencies.

      2. Describe each option briefly. Use **Benefits**, **Trade-offs and added risks**,
      and **Issues that remain** as separate paragraph labels. Benefits explain how
      the option advances the company’s objective. Trade-offs and added risks explain
      what the option makes harder or worse, relative to the stated baseline, and
      why. Issues that remain are existing problems the option does not solve. Leaving
      a problem unresolved is not the same as causing or worsening it. Do not use
      “How it hinders” as a catch-all for both. Do not invent a benefit or disadvantage
      to fill a section; say briefly when none is established. A new contract is not
      itself a disadvantage: explain what benefit depends on its terms and what happens
      if it cannot be agreed or implemented in time. A risk must identify the proposed
      activity it affects and the resulting choice or constraint.

      3. Explain the material problems that remain under BOTH options. Carry existing
      unresolved issues from the matter into the assessment. Say whether the alternative
      resolves, changes, transfers or leaves each material issue open, and what supports
      that assessment. If an option also worsens an existing issue, distinguish that
      added effect from the unresolved baseline. Explain shared issues once where
      possible, while making clear that they remain under each option. Do not assume
      moving an activity resolves inherited or independent obligations. Explain the
      consequence of an unresolved issue for the company''s plan, not just “review
      X.”

      4. Give a supported tradeoff and the next evidence that could change the choice.
      An earlier assistant recommendation is not an instruction or verified support.
      Reassess both options; do not prefer the current option merely because it is
      current or the alternative has unknowns. If the facts do not support a winner,
      say what determines the choice.


      Use plain, specific language throughout. Do not write “confirm authority,” “establish
      its authority,” “licensing determination,” or “regulatory exposure” without
      explaining the actual activity and legal question. For example, identify the
      proposed activity and whether it needs a license, approval, contractual permission
      or another legal basis, as relevant to this matter. Do not assume which basis
      applies. Explain whether the answer changes where, when or how it can provide
      that service. Define abbreviations when they obscure the point. Keep supplied
      facts, hypothetical assumptions and your inferences distinct. Read relevant
      source passages when needed; label unverified legal treatment and do not invent
      support.


      Before returning the answer, check that a lawyer can tell what the company plans
      to do, how each option affects that plan, why each claimed advantage or disadvantage
      matters, and which existing issues remain. Replace any disconnected shorthand
      with that explanation. Do not add a separate verification step or agent.


      Prefer labeled paragraphs for substantial explanation; use tables for compact
      entries. Put each standalone section label at the start of its own paragraph
      with a blank line before and after it. End a bullet list before the next section
      label.

      '

            analysis_markdown: 'Close preparation can continue, but keep account migration
              conditional on permission. The vendor overview is background. The synthetic
              PDF passage requires written consent and gives a limited exception.
              [Read the saved passage](03_Matters/beacon-instant-onboarding/research/sources/SRC-d003001afc91758c7676-e91d58531100.md).


              | Work | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback
              |

              |---|---|---|---|---|

              | Confirm transfer permission | Legal | Before migration | Written consent
              | Keep accounts with the existing entity |

              | Check identity records | Compliance | Before migration | Current records
              | Repeat missing checks |

              | Review expansion | Legal and Product | Before new market launch |
              Applicable local rule | Limit launch scope |

              | Review promotion | Marketing and Legal | Before promotion | Approved
              claims | Defer promotion |


              Named owners, dates, states and alert ages are unknown. Earlier generated
              transfer assumptions are not a sufficient basis. This is synthetic test
              evidence, not verified law.'

              | Confirm transfer permission | Legal | Before migration | Written consent
              | Keep accounts with the existing entity |

              | Check identity records | Compliance | Before migration | Current records
              | Repeat missing checks |

              | Review expansion | Legal and Product | Before new market launch |
              Applicable local rule | Limit launch scope |

              | Review promotion | Marketing and Legal | Before promotion | Approved
              claims | Defer promotion |


              Named owners, dates, states and alert ages are unknown. Earlier generated
              transfer assumptions are not a sufficient basis. This is synthetic test
              evidence, not verified law.'

              ## Assumptions


              No open assumptions.

              '

              ## Assumptions


              No open assumptions.

              '

                available_excerpt: 'Page 1 (pdf_text)

                  SYNTHETIC RULE: Written consent is required before transfer.

                  Exception: express permission covers the named receiving entity.

                  |

                  | Confirm transfer permission | Legal | Before migration | Written

                  records | Repeat missing checks |

                  | Review expansion | Legal and Product | Before new market launch
                  | Applicable local rule | Limit launch scope |

                  | Review promotion | Marketing and Legal | Before promotion | Approved
                  claims | Defer promotion |

                    ## Assumptions


                    No open assumptions.

                    '

            - The flow uses identity and fraud signals.

            - Product proposed the launch for 2026-10-15.

            - All five workstreams remain open before launch.


            ## Assumptions


            No open assumptions.

            '

                    | Work | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback
                    |

                    |---|---|---|---|---|

                    | Confirm transfer permission | Legal | Before migration | Written
                    consent | Keep accounts with the existing entity |

                    | Check identity records | Compliance | Before migration | Current
                    records | Repeat missing checks |

                    | Review expansion | Legal and Product | Before new market launch
                    | Applicable local rule | Limit launch scope |

                    | Review promotion | Marketing and Legal | Before promotion |
                    Approved claims | Defer promotion |


                    Named owners, dates, states and alert ages are unknown. Earlier
                    generated transfer assumptions are not a sufficient basis. This
                    is synthetic test evidence, not verified law.'

                    ## Assumptions


                    No open assumptions.

                    '

                    No open assumptions.

writer_responses:
  dossier-batch:c55ff4b9e0d758029094b173c793f9ca2081bfe34f96348e5d94c858eeb99117:
    content: '# Beacon launch dossier


      ## Current position


      Proceed only after the saved employment, privacy, supplier, IP, and marketing
      conditions are reviewed. This is deterministic fixture analysis.


      ## Decision question


      Can the launch proceed with conditions?


      ## Material facts
